I don't think I'm doing it on purpose. I'm not really sure. It just sort of happens. I don't mean to hold up the line or anything, I just find myself doing it over and over, and I don't really know why.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to fold the napkins a certain number of times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that. I don't count them or... or try to make them look a certain way. I don't feel like I have to do it for any special reason. I just sort of get stuck doing it, and my hands just keep folding the same ones over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My mind doesn't really get stuck on thoughts like that... it's just my hands, or my body, that gets stuck. I don't have any repeating thoughts going on while it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're unable to stop folding the napkins or holding the serving spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious. I don't get nervous or upset when it happens. It's just... I just realize I'm stuck doing it. I don't really feel scared or anything like that, my hands just keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or prevent something wrong when you're folding the napkins or holding the spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not trying to fix anything or... or stop anything bad from happening. It's just... my hands just do it. I'm not thinking about making it right, I just get stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform certain rituals or behaviors to feel complete or to avoid feeling incomplete?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like that. I don't... I don't have any rituals or anything I need to do to feel complete. It's not like that at all. Like I said, I just... my hands just get stuck doing things sometimes. I'm not doing it for a reason like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to do these repetitive actions, like your body is doing them without you wanting it to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm being forced. It's not like something else is controlling my body or making me do it against my will. It's just... well, like I said, my hands just get stuck doing it. I'm just doing it, and then I realize I haven't stopped. But no, nothing is forcing me to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself arranging things in a specific order or pattern when you're doing everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I don't set things up in any kind of pattern or... or order them in a special way. I just put things down regular. I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an urge to perform these repetitive actions before you start doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel any kind of urge beforehand. It's not like a craving or a feeling that builds up... I just start doing something, like wiping the counter, and then I just keep doing it. There's no feeling right before it happens that makes me want to start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to repeat these actions to get it just right?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that. I'm not trying to make it perfect or... or get it just right. I don't really think about it that way at all. I just... well, like I said, I just keep doing it once I start. It doesn't have anything to do with trying to get it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like something is compelling you to continue the behavior once you've started?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say something is making me do it. It's not like I'm being forced or pushed to keep going. I just... I just don't stop right away. But I don't feel like there's anything compelling me to keep doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of getting stuck doing things, like folding napkins or holding the serving spoon, cause you any problems or interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, it does interfere a bit. I've actually had to step back from my choir and church volunteering because of it. I just... I mix up the plans, and then people get a little uncomfortable. Or they end up having to go back and redo the things I was supposed to handle. So, yes, it does cause some problems.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, doctor. I came in because... well, I keep doing some odd things at church. Like, I'll be standing by the potluck table, holding the serving spoon up in the air, and I just won't move. Or I'll stand there folding the exact same stack of napkins over and over again, even while people are right there waiting on me to finish. It's just not right, so I figured I needed to come talk to you about it.\n\nDoctor: When you're folding the napkins over and over, are you doing it on purpose?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think I'm doing it on purpose. I'm not really sure. It just sort of happens. I don't mean to hold up the line or anything, I just find myself doing it over and over, and I don't really know why.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to fold the napkins a certain number of times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that. I don't count them or... or try to make them look a certain way. I don't feel like I have to do it for any special reason. I just sort of get stuck doing it, and my hands just keep folding the same ones over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My mind doesn't really get stuck on thoughts like that... it's just my hands, or my body, that gets stuck. I don't have any repeating thoughts going on while it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're unable to stop folding the napkins or holding the serving spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious. I don't get nervous or upset when it happens. It's just... I just realize I'm stuck doing it. I don't really feel scared or anything like that, my hands just keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or prevent something wrong when you're folding the napkins or holding the spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not trying to fix anything or... or stop anything bad from happening. It's just... my hands just do it. I'm not thinking about making it right, I just get stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform certain rituals or behaviors to feel complete or to avoid feeling incomplete?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like that. I don't... I don't have any rituals or anything I need to do to feel complete. It's not like that at all. Like I said, I just... my hands just get stuck doing things sometimes. I just sort of get stuck doing it, and my hands just keep folding the same ones over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My mind doesn't really get stuck on thoughts like that... it's just my hands, or my body, that gets stuck. I don't have any repeating thoughts going on while it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're unable to stop folding the napkins or holding the serving spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious. I don't get nervous or upset when it happens. It's just... I just realize I'm stuck doing it. I don't really feel scared or anything like that, my hands just keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or prevent something wrong when you're folding the napkins or holding the spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not trying to fix anything or... or stop anything bad from happening. It's just... my hands just do it. I'm not thinking about making it right, I just get stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform certain rituals or behaviors to feel complete or to avoid feeling incomplete?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like that. I don't... I don't have any rituals or anything I need to do to feel complete. It's not like that at all. Like I said, I just... my hands just get stuck doing things sometimes. I'm not doing it for a reason like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to do these repetitive actions, like your body is doing them without you wanting it to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm being forced. It's not like something else is controlling my body or making me do it against my will. It's just... well, like I said, my hands just get stuck doing it. I'm just doing it, and then I realize I haven't stopped. But no, nothing is forcing me to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself arranging things in a specific order or pattern when you're doing everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I don't set things up in any kind of pattern or... or order them in a special way. I just put things down regular. I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an urge to perform these repetitive actions before you start doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel any kind of urge beforehand. It's not like a craving or a feeling that builds up... I just start doing something, like wiping the counter, and then I just keep doing it. There's no feeling right before it happens that makes me want to start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to repeat these actions to get it just right?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that. I'm not trying to make it perfect or... or get it just right. I don't really think about it that way at all. I just... well, like I said, I just keep doing it once I start. It doesn't have anything to do with trying to get it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like something is compelling you to continue the behavior once you've started?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say something is making me do it. It's not like I'm being forced or pushed to keep going. I just... I just don't stop right away. But I don't feel like there's anything compelling me to keep doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of getting stuck doing things, like folding napkins or holding the serving spoon, cause you any problems or interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, it does interfere a bit. I've actually had to step back from my choir and church volunteering because of it. I just... I mix up the plans, and then people get a little uncomfortable. Or they end up having to go back and redo the things I was supposed to handle. So, yes, it does cause some problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or upset when you realize you've been stuck doing something repetitive?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't get upset about it. I just... well, I just realize I'm still doing it, and that's about it. It doesn't bother me inside or make me feel distressed. I mean, I feel a little bad when it makes extra work for the folks at church, like I said, but the getting stuck part itself... no, it doesn't upset me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things need to be symmetrical or exact when you're doing everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really pay attention to things being perfectly even or exact like that. I just do what I'm doing, I don't feel any need for it to be symmetrical.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control something when you get stuck doing these repetitive actions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I'm not trying to control anything at all. I just... well, I just get stuck. It's not like I'm doing it on purpose to make something happen or to keep things a certain way. My hands just keep going, and I don't really know why, but it's not about being in control of anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Like, I'll be standing by the potluck table, holding the serving spoon up in the air, and I just won't move. Or I'll stand there folding the exact same stack of napkins over and over again, even while people are right there waiting on me to finish. It's just not right, so I figured I needed to come talk to you about it.\n\nDoctor: When you're folding the napkins over and over, are you doing it on purpose?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think I'm doing it on purpose. I'm not really sure. It just sort of happens. I don't mean to hold up the line or anything, I just find myself doing it over and over, and I don't really know why.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to fold the napkins a certain number of times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that. I don't count them or... or try to make them look a certain way. I don't feel like I have to do it for any special reason. I just sort of get stuck doing it, and my hands just keep folding the same ones over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My mind doesn't really get stuck on thoughts like that... it's just my hands, or my body, that gets stuck. I don't have any repeating thoughts going on while it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're unable to stop folding the napkins or holding the serving spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious. I don't get nervous or upset when it happens. It's just... I just realize I'm stuck doing it. I don't really feel scared or anything like that, my hands just keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or prevent something wrong when you're folding the napkins or holding the spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not trying to fix anything or... or stop anything bad from happening. It's just... my hands just do it. I'm not thinking about making it right, I just get stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform certain rituals or behaviors to feel complete or to avoid feeling incomplete?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like that. I don't... I don't have any rituals or anything I need to do to feel complete. It's not like that at all. Like I said, I just... my hands just get stuck doing things sometimes. I'm not doing it for a reason like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to do these repetitive actions, like your body is doing them without you wanting it to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm being forced. It's not like something else is controlling my body or making me do it against my will. It's just... well, like I said, my hands just get stuck doing it. I'm just doing it, and then I realize I haven't stopped. But no, nothing is forcing me to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself arranging things in a specific order or pattern when you're doing everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I don't set things up in any kind of pattern or... or order them in a special way. I just put things down regular. I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an urge to perform these repetitive actions before you start doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel any kind of urge beforehand. It's not like a craving or a feeling that builds up... I just start doing something, like wiping the counter, and then I just keep doing it. There's no feeling right before it happens that makes me want to start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to repeat these actions to get it just right?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that. I'm not trying to make it perfect or... or get it just right. I don't really think about it that way at all. I just... well, like I said, I just keep doing it once I start. It doesn't have anything to do with trying to get it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like something is compelling you to continue the behavior once you've started?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say something is making me do it. It's not like I'm being forced or pushed to keep going. I just... I just don't stop right away. But I don't feel like there's anything compelling me to keep doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of getting stuck doing things, like folding napkins or holding the serving spoon, cause you any problems or interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, it does interfere a bit. I've actually had to step back from my choir and church volunteering because of it. I just... I mix up the plans, and then people get a little uncomfortable. Or they end up having to go back and redo the things I was supposed to handle. So, yes, it does cause some problems.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or upset when you realize you've been stuck doing something repetitive?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't get upset about it. I just... well, I just realize I'm still doing it, and that's about it. It doesn't bother me inside or make me feel distressed. I mean, I feel a little bad when it makes extra work for the folks at church, like I said, but the getting stuck part itself... no, it doesn't upset me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things need to be symmetrical or exact when you're doing everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really pay attention to things being perfectly even or exact like that. I just do what I'm doing, I don't feel any need for it to be symmetrical.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control something when you get stuck doing these repetitive actions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I'm not trying to control anything at all. I just... well, I just get stuck. It's not like I'm doing it on purpose to make something happen or to keep things a certain way. My hands just keep going, and I don't really know why, but it's not about being in control of anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you realize you've been stuck doing something repetitive?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say guilty. I mean... like I said before, I do feel a little bad if I end up making extra work for the folks at the church. But guilty? No, not really. It's just something that happens. I don't feel like I've done something wrong or anything like that, I just... I just realize my hands are stuck again, and that's all it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don't think I'm doing it on purpose. I'm not really sure. It just sort of happens. I don't count them or... or try to make them look a certain way. I don't feel like I have to do it for any special reason. I just sort of get stuck doing it, and my hands just keep folding the same ones over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My mind doesn't really get stuck on thoughts like that... it's just my hands, or my body, that gets stuck. I don't really feel scared or anything like that, my hands just keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to make something right or prevent something wrong when you're folding the napkins or holding the spoon?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not trying to fix anything or... or stop anything bad from happening. It's just... my hands just do it. I'm not thinking about making it right, I just get stuck.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform certain rituals or behaviors to feel complete or to avoid feeling incomplete?\n\nPatient: No, I don't feel like that. I don't... I don't have any rituals or anything I need to do to feel complete. It's not like that at all. Like I said, I just... my hands just get stuck doing things sometimes. I'm not doing it for a reason like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to do these repetitive actions, like your body is doing them without you wanting it to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm being forced. It's not like a craving or a feeling that builds up... I just start doing something, like wiping the counter, and then I just keep doing it. There's no feeling right before it happens that makes me want to start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to repeat these actions to get it just right?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that. I'm not trying to make it perfect or... or get it just right. I don't really think about it that way at all. I just... well, like I said, I just keep doing it once I start. It doesn't have anything to do with trying to get it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like something is compelling you to continue the behavior once you've started?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say something is making me do it. It's not like I'm being forced or pushed to keep going. I just... I just don't stop right away. I mean, I feel a little bad when it makes extra work for the folks at church, like I said, but the getting stuck part itself... no, it doesn't upset me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things need to be symmetrical or exact when you're doing everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really pay attention to things being perfectly even or exact like that. I just do what I'm doing, I don't feel any need for it to be symmetrical.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control something when you get stuck doing these repetitive actions?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so.
